# Turkey

<section class="hmi-quick-read" id="turkey-quick-read" data-hmi-quick-read aria-labelledby="turkey-quick-read-label"><div class="hmi-quick-read-label" id="turkey-quick-read-label">Quick read</div>

Turkey in the HMI library has 78 reported results for lead, cadmium, total arsenic, inorganic arsenic, total mercury, nickel, chromium, aluminium, tin, uranium, Co, Cu, Zn, Se, As_nonextractable, AsIII, AsV and CrIII from 9 sources.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World from 2017–2020.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration Cantoral et al. 2024 reported lead at 26 µg/kg (mean ± 10 SD) in turkey, as sold from Meats, sausages, and eggs (retail, Mexico City).<sup class="cite">[[sources/cantoral2024-lead-levels-mexican-foods|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Cantoral A, Betanzos-Robledo L, Collado-López S, García-Martínez BA, Lamadrid-Figueroa H, Mariscal-Moreno RM, Díaz-Ruiz A, Ríos C, Téllez-Rojo MM · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/cantoral2024-lead-levels-mexican-foods">Lead Levels in the Most Consumed Mexican Foods: First Monitoring Effort</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Toxics</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/cantoral2024-lead-levels-mexican-foods">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Raeeszadeh et al. 2021 reported cadmium at 290 µg/kg (mean ± 130 SD) and total arsenic at 880 µg/kg (mean ± 210 SD) in turkey, as sold from Sanandaj distribution centers.<sup class="cite">[[sources/raeeszadeh2021-animal-meat-metals-iran|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Raeeszadeh M, Gravandi H, Akbari A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/raeeszadeh2021-animal-meat-metals-iran">Determination of some heavy metals concentration in species animal meat (sheep, beef, turkey, and ostrich) and carcinogenic health risk assessment in Kurdistan province, western Iran</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Research Square</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/raeeszadeh2021-animal-meat-metals-iran">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

For a buyer the practical points are the form bought, the basis a result is reported on, where the turkey came from and the laboratory’s reporting limit. Testing finds contamination but does not remove it.



<a id="ingredient-identity-and-scope"></a>
## What counts as turkey

<a id="hmi-ing-001"></a>
<a id="what-commodity-biological-species-edible-part-and-commercial-forms-does-this-page-cover"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-002"></a>
<a id="which-processing-states-and-derivatives-are-distinct-materials-rather-than-synonyms"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-003"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-can-be-transferred-between-those-materials-and-what-requires-a-separate-answer"></a>

<div class="hmi-analyte-explorer" id="hmi-occurrence-turkey" data-analyte-explorer data-compact-intro="true" data-source-summaries="true"><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-explorer-context><summary data-open-context aria-haspopup="dialog" aria-controls="hmi-occurrence-turkey-drawer">Comparison methods and scope</summary><div data-explorer-context-content>

<p>Each row is one statistic a source reports for one form of turkey, group and metal; percentiles, ranges and maxima reported for the same samples as a mean or median are shown on that row. Different forms and preparation states are kept apart, and dry-weight results never share a reference with as-sold or fresh-weight results. Ratings place a result against an HMI reference built from monitoring results and ordinary-setting medians for the same form and basis. Same-study contrasts compare a contaminated or treated site with its control, targeted with random sampling, a prepared form with the unprocessed one, or one group of a study with the median of its other groups. Held rows failed a check against their own source and are shown but not compared.</p>

Turkey, as sold; As sold, as eaten or fresh weight, µg/kg; no moisture conversion. Distribution of subgroup median concentration values.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Reference (µg/kg)</th><th scope="col">Q1</th><th scope="col">Median</th><th scope="col">Q3</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Primary HMI reference</td><td>≤5.38</td><td>≤7</td><td>≤20</td></tr><tr><td>Alternative weighting</td><td>≤5.8</td><td>≤7</td><td>≤20</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>49 reference units; 1 sampling family. Counts describe evidence units, not independent studies or certainty.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Contributing family</th><th scope="col">Reference units</th><th scope="col">Weight</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>WHO GEMS/Food</td><td>49</td><td>100%</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Methods</p><p>HMI pools subgroup medians for turkey, as sold on a as sold, as eaten or fresh weight basis: monitoring results summarized as one median per reporting region, sampling type and collection year, total diet composites and printed sample values as single observations, and printed subgroup medians from ordinary farms or markets. Each sampling family has equal total weight. Non-detects stay as zero-to-limit intervals, so quartiles carry lower and upper bounds. Means, ranges and maxima never enter as observations. Other forms, other bases, targeted sampling and results from mining, industrial, wastewater-affected, experimental, fertiliser or manufacturer settings are kept out of this reference.</p><p>Inverse weighted empirical CDF; midpoint at exact cumulative-weight boundaries. At least four reference units and a definitely positive Q1 to Q3 spread are required. This is a display eligibility rule, not statistical validation.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Limitations</p><p>WHO GEMS/Food is the only compatible sampling family for turkey, as sold on this basis in the completed turkey ledger, so this single-family reference cannot represent other markets. Reporting regions and purchase markets are not growing areas. Non-detects with differing limits widen the bounds. A position is descriptive concentration, not a safety or compliance judgment.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Admitted source families</p><ul><li><a href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" class="internal" data-slug="sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" data-no-cite-meta>WHO GEMS/Food</a></li></ul><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reference version and interpretation</summary><p>Frozen evidence selection: 2026-10-22. It describes the evidence currently in this scope; it is not a worldwide or current-lot benchmark. Source, year and region filters do not change it. Method: hmi-pooled-reference-v2.</p><p>hmi-pooled-reference-v2:turkey-f7aad3634a19:2924d14045a7</p><p>The reference is computed by HMI from admitted evidence, not copied from a study baseline. It describes the evidence currently in this scope; it is not a worldwide or current-lot benchmark. Source, year and region filters do not change it. Different HMI scopes are not one common ranking.</p></details></div></details><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-baseline-fallback id="hmi-reference-hmi-pooled-reference-v2:turkey-65cf19f5cd68:ec8d335413ba"><summary>HMI turkey Sn reference · Turkey, as sold</summary><div data-baseline-reference-template="hmi-pooled-reference-v2:turkey-65cf19f5cd68:ec8d335413ba"><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Reference and scope</p><p>HMI turkey Sn reference · Turkey, as sold. Turkey, as sold; As sold, as eaten or fresh weight, µg/kg; no moisture conversion. Distribution of subgroup median concentration values.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Reference (µg/kg)</th><th scope="col">Q1</th><th scope="col">Median</th><th scope="col">Q3</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Primary HMI reference</td><td>6.25</td><td>7.89</td><td>9.29</td></tr><tr><td>Alternative weighting</td><td>6.25</td><td>7.89</td><td>9.29</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>3 reference units; 1 sampling family. Counts describe evidence units, not independent studies or certainty.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Contributing family</th><th scope="col">Reference units</th><th scope="col">Weight</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>WHO GEMS/Food</td><td>3</td><td>100%</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Methods</p><p>HMI pools subgroup medians for turkey, as sold on a as sold, as eaten or fresh weight basis: monitoring results summarized as one median per reporting region, sampling type and collection year, total diet composites and printed sample values as single observations, and printed subgroup medians from ordinary farms or markets. Each sampling family has equal total weight. Non-detects stay as zero-to-limit intervals, so quartiles carry lower and upper bounds. Means, ranges and maxima never enter as observations. Other forms, other bases, targeted sampling and results from mining, industrial, wastewater-affected, experimental, fertiliser or manufacturer settings are kept out of this reference.</p><p>Inverse weighted empirical CDF; midpoint at exact cumulative-weight boundaries. At least four reference units and a definitely positive Q1 to Q3 spread are required. This is a display eligibility rule, not statistical validation.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Limitations</p><p>WHO GEMS/Food is the only compatible sampling family for turkey, as sold on this basis in the completed turkey ledger, so this single-family reference cannot represent other markets. Reporting regions and purchase markets are not growing areas. Non-detects with differing limits widen the bounds. A position is descriptive concentration, not a safety or compliance judgment.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Admitted source families</p><ul><li><a href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" class="internal" data-slug="sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" data-no-cite-meta>WHO GEMS/Food</a></li></ul><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reference version and interpretation</summary><p>Frozen evidence selection: 2026-10-22. data-evidence-content><p class="hmi-dataset-title"><a href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" class="internal" data-slug="sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">WHO GEMS/Food</a></p><p>Turkey, as sold · Lead (Pb) · 44 results.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Selected finding and reference</p><div class="hmi-selected-result" data-selected-finding><strong class="hmi-finding">median 2.34–4.67 µg/kg (bounds)</strong><span class="hmi-row-note">median.</span><p>44 results. Basis: As sold, as eaten or fresh weight.</p><p>Non-detects are intervals from zero to the reporting limit; never a measured zero</p></div><div class="hmi-selected-comparison" data-selected-comparison><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">HMI baseline position</p><p>Limited data · HMI turkey Pb reference · Turkey, as sold. median as reported: 2.34–4.67 (bounded) µg/kg. HMI median ≤7; Q1 ≤5.38; Q3 ≤20 µg/kg. 1 family. Reporting bounds prevent a rating.</p><p>Reporting bounds or insufficient reference spread prevent a stable Low / Moderate / High classification.</p><p>This finding’s sampling family contributes to the fixed reference.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Sensitivity to weighting</p><p>Equal weight per subgroup: Limited data. The position remains unresolved under this alternative. This check does not address all sampling, rounding or analytical uncertainty.</p><div data-baseline-reference="hmi-pooled-reference-v2:turkey-f6d62d014285:e7382704edc1"><a href="#hmi-reference-hmi-pooled-reference-v2:turkey-f6d62d014285:e7382704edc1">Reference, contributing evidence and methods</a></div></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Collection period</p><p data-timeline-scope>2007–2019</p><div data-selected-timeline><p>No year-by-year sample register is reconstructed from this summary.</p></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Geographic scope</p><p data-regional-scope>EURO</p><div data-selected-regional><p>Purchase or reporting market; growing area not identified.</p></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Sampling, analysis and reporting limits</p><p>As described by the monitoring programme.</p><p>Non-detects are intervals from zero to the reporting limit; never a measured zero</p><p></p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Shared samples and study overlap</p><p>Subgroup rows from the same source share its laboratory and sampling frame; they are not independent studies.</p><p>Each table row describes a food/analyte result. Different HMI scopes are not one common ranking.</p></details></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Collection period</p><p data-timeline-scope>2018–2020</p><div data-selected-timeline><p>No year-by-year sample register is reconstructed from this summary.</p></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Geographic scope</p><p data-regional-scope>all samples</p><div data-selected-regional><p>All samples in the study.</p></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Sampling, analysis and reporting limits</p><p>Microwave digestion, ICP-MS (Gray et al. 2020 method cited).</p><p>No censoring reported</p><p>TDS food no. 390; hybrid mean 1.1, lower bound 0.8, upper bound 1.3 µg/kg; reporting limit 1 µg/kg; 27 composite samples. Hybrid: &lt;RL set to zero if no detections 2013-2020, else RL/2. Some samples below RL, so the mean includes substituted values.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Shared samples and study overlap</p><p>Subgroup rows from the same source share its laboratory and sampling frame; they are not independent studies.</p><p>Each table row describes a food/analyte result. Repeated rows do not add independent samples or datasets. No cross-study trend is calculated in this comparison mode.</p><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reported values, conversion and source locator</summary><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Source statistic</th><th scope="col">Original result</th><th scope="col">Displayed unit conversion</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>mean</td><td>Hybrid mean 1.1 µg/kg (LB 0.8, UB 1.3)</td><td>as recorded in the findings store</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>Table A2, TDS food 390.</p><p>No moisture, cooking-yield or species conversion is applied to the original finding above. (upper quartile).<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug <a id="mitigation-options"></a>
## Mitigation for turkey

<a id="hmi-ing-019"></a>

Lot testing against the applicable limit is the check that can be verified.

<p class="hmi-mitigation-gap" data-mitigation-gap>No measured way to lower metals in this food is in the library yet.</p>



<a id="trade-offs-and-failure-modes"></a>
## Trade-offs and limits of controls

<a id="hmi-ing-022"></a>
<a id="can-an-intervention-increase-another-contaminant-or-redistribute-contamination-to-a-by-product"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-023"></a>
<a id="what-effects-on-nutrition-yield-sensory-quality-stability-and-waste-streams-were-measured"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-024"></a>
<a id="what-costs-constraints-adverse-effects-or-failure-modes-are-documented-and-which-are-unknown"></a>

Metals do not move together, so a lot that passes a lead limit says little about cadmium, arsenic or nickel unless those are tested as well.



<a id="sampling-and-testing"></a>
## Testing turkey for metals

<a id="hmi-ing-025"></a>
<a id="what-sampling-design-is-appropriate-for-the-ingredient-heterogeneity-lot-and-decision"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-026"></a>
<a id="which-methods-species-measurements-validation-evidence-and-reporting-limits-are-needed"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-027"></a>
<a id="what-can-a-certificate-of-analysis-or-non-detect-establish-and-what-remains-untested"></a>

Results depend on the method and its reporting limit. Reporting limits for lead ranged from 2 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food) to 3,000 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Reporting limits for cadmium ranged from 0.4 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food) to 1,000 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Reporting limits for total arsenic ranged from 3 µg/kg (FDA Total Diet Study) to 100 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a References identify the sources used in these answers. The Sources index preserves additional discovery links; inclusion there does not mean a source has been reviewed for this assessment. Corrections, completed searches, new direct evidence and changes in requirements should reopen the relevant answer.


<a id="universal-faqs"></a>
## Turkey FAQs

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-turkey" data-anchor="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-turkey" data-question="What heavy metals are found in turkey?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>What heavy metals are found in turkey?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

The library reports lead, cadmium, total arsenic, inorganic arsenic, total mercury, nickel, chromium, aluminium, tin, uranium, Co, Cu, Zn, Se, As_nonextractable, AsIII, AsV and CrIII in turkey, most often lead, cadmium and total arsenic.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/raeeszadeh2021-animal-meat-metals-iran|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/cantoral2024-lead-levels-mexican-foods|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Cantoral A, Betanzos-Robledo L, Collado-López S, García-Martínez BA, Lamadrid-Figueroa H, Mariscal-Moreno RM, Díaz-Ruiz A, Ríos C, Téllez-Rojo MM · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" <details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="how-much-of-the-arsenic-in-turkey-is-inorganic" data-anchor="how-much-of-the-arsenic-in-turkey-is-inorganic" data-question="How much of the arsenic in turkey is inorganic?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>How much of the arsenic in turkey is inorganic?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Inorganic arsenic, the more toxic form, was measured in 2 results from 2 sources, far fewer than total arsenic.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs|7]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">European Food Safety Authority (EFSA) · 2014</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs">Dietary exposure to inorganic arsenic in the European population</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">EFSA Journal 2014;12(3):3597</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> European Food Safety Authority (EFSA) 2014 reported inorganic arsenic at 7.5 µg/kg (mean) in turkey, as sold from European dietary-survey occurrence data.<sup class="cite">[[sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs|7]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">European Food Safety Authority (EFSA) · 2014</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs">Dietary exposure to inorganic arsenic in the European population</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">EFSA Journal 2014;12(3):3597</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>
